1	# RL Lab — Spec
2	
3	**Status:** Approved · **Last updated:** 2026-10-17 · **Brief:** [brief.md](brief.md)
4	
5	## Summary
6	A set of small 2D reinforcement-learning worlds (Snake, Fish tank, Predator-prey) built on one shared core: a world interface, a training loop, logging with learning-curve charts, a replay viewer, and baseline evaluation. In each world a hand-written learning agent is trained on a laptop, checked against random and rule-based play, watched in replays, and explained in a write-up. See the [brief](brief.md) for why.
7	
8	## Users
9	| User | What they need to do |
10	|---|---|
11	| The learner (sole user) | Add a world, train agents in it on a laptop, check they beat the baselines, watch replays across training, and write up why the method works and where it struggles |
12	
13	## Scope
14	**In scope:** World interface, Training runs, Run logging & learning curves, Replay viewer, Baseline evaluation, Reference check, World write-ups, Snake, Fish tank, Predator-prey.
15	
16	**Out of scope:**
17	- 3D physics.
18	- Learning from screen pixels, or Atari games.
19	- Reproducing research papers or matching published numbers.
20	- Web deployment or a hosted demo; everything runs locally.
21	- Chasing top scores; clearly beating the baselines is enough.
22	- Hyperparameter search beyond what the learner tunes by hand.
23	- Written explanations generated for the learner; write-ups are the learner's own words.
24	
25	## Features
26	
27	### World interface (`WORLD`)
28	**Serves:** Goal 4
29	**Stage:** S1
30	**Behavior:** Every world presents the same face to the rest of the system: it can be reset from a seed, it reports what each agent observes as a short list of numbers, it accepts each agent's action, and it returns rewards, an end-of-episode signal and a world-specific score. It also describes its current state in a form the replay viewer can draw.
31	
32	**Requirements**
33	- **WORLD-01** A world starts a new episode from a seed, and the same seed always gives the same starting state.
34	  - Given a world and seed 42, when two episodes are started, then both starting states are identical.
35	- **WORLD-02** Given the same seed and the same sequence of actions, a world produces the same sequence of observations, rewards and end signals.
36	  - Given a recorded episode's seed and actions, when they are replayed into a fresh world, then every observation, reward and end signal matches the recording.
37	- **WORLD-03** Each agent's observation is a fixed-length list of numbers, never an image.
38	  - Given any world, when an observation is taken at any step, then it has the same length as at every other step of that world and variant.
39	- **WORLD-04** A world declares its agents, the observation length and the action format (a choice from a fixed set, or a list of numbers within stated limits) before any episode starts.
40	  - Given any world, when the training loop asks for its description, then it receives the agent list, observation length and action format without running an episode.
41	- **WORLD-05** Every world reports a per-episode score with a stated direction (higher is better, or lower is better), used by evaluation and charts.
42	  - Given a finished episode, when its score is requested, then a single number and the world's direction are returned.
43	- **WORLD-06** A world reports its drawable state at every step (positions, sizes and kinds of every object) for the replay viewer.
44	  - Given any step of any episode, when the drawable state is requested, then every object on screen is listed with its position.
45	- **WORLD-07** A world can hold more than one agent, each agent belonging to a named team.
46	  - Given a world with agents on two teams, when a step is taken, then each agent receives its own observation and reward, labelled with its team.
47	
48	**Edge cases & errors**
49	- An action outside the declared format or limits → the step is refused with an error naming the agent and the bad value; it is never silently clipped.
50	- An agent that has left the episode (e.g. a caught prey) is sent an action → the action is ignored and the agent receives no further observations that episode.
51	
52	### Training runs (`TRAIN`)
53	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 4
54	**Stage:** S1
55	**Behavior:** The learner starts a training run by naming a world, its variant, the learning method's settings and a seed. The run trains for a fixed budget of environment steps, saves checkpoints of the agent as it goes, and runs periodic evaluations. The same loop runs every world without change.
56	
57	**Requirements**
58	- **TRAIN-01** A training run is started from a world, a variant, the method's settings and a seed, and runs for a stated budget of environment steps.
59	  - Given snake, its default settings, seed 1 and a budget of N steps, when the run is started, then it stops after exactly N environment steps.
60	- **TRAIN-02** The same world, settings and seed on the same machine give an identical training run.
61	  - Given two runs with identical inputs, when both finish, then their logged results and final checkpoints are identical.
62	- **TRAIN-03** A run saves a checkpoint at every 10% of its budget, from the start (untrained) to the end.
63	  - Given a finished run, when its checkpoints are listed, then there are 11, at 0%, 10%, … 100% of the budget.
64	- **TRAIN-04** A run evaluates its current agent at every 5% of its budget on 20 episodes whose seeds are never used in training.
65	  - Given a finished run, when its evaluation log is read, then there are 21 evaluation points, each the mean score of 20 held-out-seed episodes.
66	- **TRAIN-05** Several runs of the same world with different seeds can be started together, one per processor core.
67	  - Given 5 seeds, when a batch of runs is started, then 5 runs proceed at the same time and each finishes with its own log and checkpoints.
68	- **TRAIN-06** Every learning method, before it is used in a world, learns the known best behavior in a tiny check world whose best behavior is worked out by hand.
69	  - Given a learning method and its check world, when it is trained for the check budget, then the trained agent picks the hand-derived best action in at least 95% of check-world states.
70	- **TRAIN-07** In worlds with more than one team, each team has its own learning method and settings, and all teams learn during the same run.
71	  - Given predator-prey with a learning method for each team, when a run proceeds, then both teams' agents change between consecutive checkpoints.
72	
73	**Edge cases & errors**
74	- A run is stopped before its budget ends → it cannot be resumed; it is marked incomplete, keeps its checkpoints so far, and is left out of learning-curve charts and evaluation unless the learner includes it by name.
75	- A run's training produces an invalid number (not-a-number or infinite) in the agent → the run stops at once, saves the last good checkpoint, and records the step and the value.
76	- A run would overwrite an existing run with the same name → it is refused; the learner picks a new name or deletes the old run first.
77	
78	### Run logging & learning curves (`LOG`)
79	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 4
80	**Stage:** S1
81	**Behavior:** Every run records its settings and results as it trains. From a group of runs of the same world and variant, the learner gets a learning-curve chart showing the agent against both baselines, across all runs.
82	
83	**Requirements**
84	- **LOG-01** Every run records its world, variant, method settings, seed, budget, start and end times, and whether it finished.
85	  - Given any finished run, when its record is opened, then every one of those fields is present.
86	- **LOG-02** Every run logs, for each training episode, the episode's score, length and total reward per team.
87	  - Given a finished run, when its episode log is read, then it has one entry per training episode with those values.
88	- **LOG-03** For a world and variant with at least 3 finished runs, a learning-curve chart shows evaluation score against environment steps: the mean across runs as a line, the lowest-to-highest range across runs as a band, and each baseline's mean score as a horizontal reference line.
89	  - Given 5 finished snake runs, when the chart is made, then it shows one mean line, one band, a random-play line and a rule-based line, with the axes labelled with the score name and "environment steps".
90	- **LOG-04** Learning-curve charts follow the `dataviz` skill's guidance for form, colour and labelling.
91	  - Given any learning-curve chart, when it is reviewed against the `dataviz` skill, then it passes that skill's checks.
92	- **LOG-05** Each world can add its own logged measures (e.g. snake length at death, hits per fish), which are charted the same way as the score.
93	  - Given a world that declares an extra measure, when its chart is requested, then it is drawn with the same mean line and range band.
94	- **LOG-06** The learner can view the latest logged values of a run while it trains.
95	  - Given a run in progress, when the learner checks it, then they see the current step, the latest evaluation score and the time elapsed, no more than 1 evaluation point behind.
96	
97	**Edge cases & errors**
98	- Runs with different budgets or settings are asked for in one chart → refused, naming the differing settings; one chart only combines runs that differ by seed.
99	- Fewer than 3 finished runs → the chart is still drawn, but titled with the run count and a note that the band is not meaningful.
100	
101	### Replay viewer (`REPLAY`)
102	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 4
103	**Stage:** S1
104	**Behavior:** The learner picks a run, one of its checkpoints and a seed, and watches that agent play an episode in a local window. They can pause, step and change speed, and put two checkpoints side by side on the same starting state to see how training changed the behavior.
105	
106	**Requirements**
107	- **REPLAY-01** The learner can watch an episode played by any checkpoint of any run, from a seed they choose.
108	  - Given a snake run's 50% checkpoint and seed 7, when the replay is opened, then the episode plays from its first step to its end.
109	- **REPLAY-02** Replays are exact: the same checkpoint and seed always show the same episode.
110	  - Given a checkpoint and seed, when the replay is played twice, then every frame matches.
111	- **REPLAY-03** The replay can be paused, stepped one step forward or back, and played at 0.25×, 1×, 4× or 16× speed.
112	  - Given a paused replay at step 30, when the learner steps forward, then step 31 is shown; when they step back twice, step 29 is shown.
113	- **REPLAY-04** Two checkpoints can be shown side by side on the same starting state, stepping in sync.
114	  - Given the 10% and 100% snake checkpoints and seed 7, when compared, then both start from the same board and advance one step at a time together; when one episode ends, its last frame stays while the other continues.
115	- **REPLAY-05** The replay shows the current step, the running score and each agent's latest reward next to the world.
116	  - Given any frame, when it is shown, then the step number, the score so far and the latest rewards are visible.
117	- **REPLAY-06** The learner can also watch the random-play and rule-based baselines in the same viewer, including side by side with a checkpoint.
118	  - Given the rule-based snake and the final checkpoint on seed 7, when compared, then both play side by side as in REPLAY-04.
119	- **REPLAY-07** A replay can be saved as a video file to keep alongside the write-up.
120	  - Given an open replay, when the learner saves it, then a video of the whole episode at 1× speed is written and plays in a standard video player.
121	
122	- **REPLAY-08** In worlds where agents see only nearby things, the replay can show each agent's sight radius as an overlay, switched on or off by the learner.
123	  - Given a local-sight fish replay, when the overlay is switched on, then a circle of the sight radius is drawn around every fish; when switched off, the circles disappear.
124	
125	**Edge cases & errors**
126	- A checkpoint from a world or variant whose observation length or action format no longer matches → the replay refuses to open and names the mismatch.
127	- An episode that never ends on its own → the replay stops at the world's step limit and says so.
128	
129	### Baseline evaluation (`EVAL`)
130	**Serves:** Goal 1
131	**Stage:** S1
132	**Behavior:** For every world and variant there are two baselines: random play and a simple hand-written rule. A final evaluation plays every run's final checkpoint and both baselines on the same held-out seeds and tests whether the learned agent beats each baseline by more than chance.
133	
134	**Requirements**
135	- **EVAL-01** Every world and variant has a random-play baseline that picks each action uniformly from the allowed actions.
136	  - Given any world, when the random baseline plays 100 steps, then every action it takes is within the declared action format.
137	- **EVAL-02** Every world and variant has a rule-based baseline that the learner writes by hand, described in one sentence in the world's feature below.
138	  - Given any world, when its rule baseline is listed, then its one-sentence description is shown with it.
139	- **EVAL-03** Final evaluation plays every final checkpoint and both baselines on the same 100 seeds, none of which was used in training or in-training evaluation.
140	  - Given 5 finished runs, when final evaluation is run, then 700 episodes are played (5 × 100 learned, 100 random, 100 rule-based), all on the same 100 held-out seeds.
141	- **EVAL-04** Whether the learned agent beats each baseline is tested with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill. It is given: the hypothesis "the trained agent scores better than the baseline"; H0 = the learned agent's mean score is no better than the baseline's, H1 = it is better (in the world's score direction); the statistic = difference in mean episode score, learned (averaged over the runs, per seed) minus baseline; the null built by randomly swapping the learned and baseline labels within each held-out seed (paired permutation, 10,000 permutations); α = 0.05 with Holm correction across all baseline comparisons in that world (2 per variant); the EVAL-03 held-out episodes. The learned agent passes only if both nulls are rejected.
142	  - Given final evaluation results for one variant, when tested, then a report shows, for each baseline, the mean scores, the difference, the corrected p-value and reject/keep.
143	- **EVAL-05** "Clearly beats" also needs every single run to beat the rule baseline: each run's final mean score is better than the rule-based baseline's mean score.
144	  - Given 5 runs where 4 beat the rule baseline and 1 does not, when the verdict is made, then the world is marked "not yet beating the baselines" and names the failing run.
145	- **EVAL-06** Final evaluation produces one summary per world and variant: mean and range of learned scores across runs, both baseline means, the EVAL-04 test results and the EVAL-05 verdict.
146	  - Given a finished evaluation, when the summary is opened, then all of those values are present.
147	
148	**Edge cases & errors**
149	- Fewer than 5 finished runs → the test is not run and the summary says how many more runs are needed.
150	- A baseline scores better than the learned agent → reported plainly as a failed result, never hidden or re-run with new seeds.
151	
152	### Reference check (`CHECK`)
153	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 3
154	**Stage:** S1
155	**Behavior:** After the hand-written method works in a world, the learner trains an established reference implementation of the same kind of method on the same world and budget, as a check that the hand-written version has no silent bugs.
156	
157	**Requirements**
158	- **CHECK-01** The learning algorithms (how actions are chosen, what is learned from each experience, and how the agent is updated) are written by the learner; automatic gradient computation may be used, but an established reference implementation of a learning method is used only for this check, never inside training runs.
159	  - Given any training run, when it runs, then no outside reinforcement-learning code takes part in it.
160	- **CHECK-02** For each world and variant, 5 runs of a reference implementation of the same kind of method are trained on the same world, budget and seeds as the hand-written method, and evaluated as in EVAL-03.
161	  - Given a finished reference check, when its summary is opened, then it shows the reference's mean and range next to the hand-written method's.
162	- **CHECK-03** The hand-written method passes the check if its mean final score is at least 80% of the reference's (or at most 125% for lower-is-better scores).
163	  - Given a hand-written snake mean of 18 food and a reference mean of 20, when checked, then it passes; at 15 against 20, it is flagged for investigation.
164	
165	**Edge cases & errors**
166	- The hand-written method scores far better than the reference (more than 150% of it) → also flagged, since it may point to a bug in scoring or evaluation rather than a better method.
167	
168	### World write-ups (`NOTES`)
169	**Serves:** Goal 3
170	**Stage:** S1
171	**Behavior:** Each world has a short write-up, written by the learner, that explains why its learning method works and where it struggles, pointing to the charts and replays that show it.
172	
173	**Requirements**
174	- **NOTES-01** Each world has a write-up with three parts: how the learning method works, why it works in this world, and where it struggles.
175	  - Given a finished world, when its write-up is opened, then all three parts are present and non-empty.
176	- **NOTES-02** The "where it struggles" part points to at least one saved replay or chart that shows the struggle.
177	  - Given a write-up, when its struggle part is read, then it names at least one saved replay or chart that exists.
178	- **NOTES-03** Write-ups are the learner's own words; nothing in the system writes or fills them in.
179	  - Given a new world, when it is set up, then its write-up starts with only the three part headings.
180	
181	**Edge cases & errors**
182	- A write-up points to a replay or chart that no longer exists → the world's finish check lists the broken link.
183	
184	### Snake (`SNAKE`)
185	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2, Goal 3
186	**Stage:** S1
187	**Behavior:** The classic snake game on a square grid. The snake moves one cell per step, turns left, right or keeps going, grows when it eats food, and dies when it hits a wall or itself. The learner trains a snake that eats steadily and avoids itself, and watches it do so.
188	
189	**Requirements**
190	- **SNAKE-01** The world is a 10 × 10 grid with one snake starting 3 cells long in the middle, and one food item on a random free cell.
191	  - Given seed 3, when an episode starts, then the snake is 3 cells long near the center and one food item is on a cell the snake does not cover.
192	- **SNAKE-02** Each step the snake chooses one of three actions: turn left, turn right or go straight.
193	  - Given the snake heading up, when it turns left, then its head moves one cell left.
194	- **SNAKE-03** Eating food grows the snake by one cell, adds 1 to the score and places new food on a random free cell.
195	  - Given the head moves onto food, when the step ends, then the snake is one cell longer, the score is up by 1 and new food is on a free cell.
196	- **SNAKE-04** The episode ends when the head hits a wall or the snake's body, or after 100 steps without eating.
197	  - Given the head moves into its own body, when the step ends, then the episode is over.
198	- **SNAKE-05** The score is the food eaten in the episode (higher is better).
199	  - Given an episode where 12 food items were eaten, when it ends, then its score is 12.
200	- **SNAKE-06** The snake observes a fixed list of numbers: danger straight ahead, left and right; its heading; the food's direction relative to its head; and its length.
201	  - Given any step, when the observation is taken, then it has the same length and holds those values.
202	- **SNAKE-07** The rule-based baseline moves toward the food along the shorter axis, unless that move would end the episode next step, in which case it takes any move that does not.
203	  - Given the food to the right and a free cell to the right, when the rule acts, then it moves right.
204	- **SNAKE-08** The trained snake beats both baselines as in EVAL-04 and EVAL-05.
205	  - Given 5 finished snake runs, when evaluated, then both baselines are beaten.
206	
207	**Edge cases & errors**
208	- The snake fills every free cell → the episode ends as a win and the score counts all food eaten.
209	- Turning back into its own neck is impossible by construction, since actions are relative turns.
210	
211	### Fish tank (`FISH`)
212	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2, Goal 3, Goal 4
213	**Stage:** S2
214	**Behavior:** A 2D tank with walls and a ball that bounces around at constant speed. Fish move smoothly by choosing how hard to push in each direction, and lose points when the ball hits them. It is built in three variants: one fish that sees everything, many fish that see everything, and many fish that only see nearby things.
215	
216	**Requirements**
217	- **FISH-01** The tank is a square with walls; the ball moves in straight lines at constant speed and bounces off walls.
218	  - Given the ball heading into the right wall, when it touches the wall, then its sideways direction reverses and its speed is unchanged.
219	- **FISH-02** Each step a fish chooses a push as two numbers between −1 and 1 (left-right and up-down); its speed changes smoothly with the push, up to a top speed below the ball's.
220	  - Given a still fish pushing (1, 0) for several steps, when its speed is read, then it rises step by step toward its top speed, never above it.
221	- **FISH-03** When the ball touches a fish, the hit is counted and the fish reappears at a random spot at least one quarter of the tank's width from the ball.
222	  - Given the ball touching a fish, when the step ends, then the hit count is up by 1 and the fish is at least a quarter-width from the ball.
223	- **FISH-04** An episode lasts 1,000 steps; the score is hits per fish per episode (lower is better).
224	  - Given 4 fish and 6 total hits in an episode, when it ends, then the score is 1.5.
225	- **FISH-05** Variant "one fish": a single fish observes its own position and velocity and the ball's position and velocity.
226	  - Given the one-fish variant, when an observation is taken, then it holds those values for the fish and the ball.
227	- **FISH-06** Variant "many fish": 8 fish share the tank; each observes itself, the ball and the 3 nearest other fish.
228	  - Given the many-fish variant, when an observation is taken for any fish, then it holds its own state, the ball's and the 3 nearest fish's.
229	- **FISH-07** Variant "local sight": as "many fish", but a fish sees the ball and other fish only within a radius of one quarter of the tank's width; anything outside is reported as "not seen".
230	  - Given the ball beyond the radius, when a fish's observation is taken, then the ball's entries read "not seen".
231	- **FISH-08** Fish pass through each other; only the ball can hit them.
232	  - Given two fish moving onto the same spot, when the step ends, then both are there and no hit is counted.
233	- **FISH-09** The rule-based baseline pushes each fish directly away from the ball at full strength (from the ball it sees, or randomly when it sees none).
234	  - Given the ball to the fish's left, when the rule acts, then the push is (1, 0).
235	- **FISH-10** Every variant's trained fish beat both baselines as in EVAL-04 and EVAL-05.
236	  - Given 5 finished runs of each variant, when evaluated, then both baselines are beaten in each variant.
237	- **FISH-11** Fish tank runs through the same training runs, logging, replay viewer and evaluation as snake, and snake's results reproduce unchanged after fish tank is added.
238	  - Given a snake run repeated with its original settings and seed after fish tank exists, when it finishes, then its results match the original run exactly.
239	
240	**Edge cases & errors**
241	- A fish pushes into a wall → it stops at the wall and slides along it; it never leaves the tank.
242	- The ball starts touching a fish → the episode's start is re-drawn so that every fish starts at least a quarter-width from the ball.
243	
244	### Predator-prey (`PREY`)
245	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2, Goal 3, Goal 4
246	**Stage:** S3
247	**Behavior:** A 2D arena with food pellets, a team of prey and a team of predators, all moving smoothly as fish do. Prey score by eating food and are removed when caught; predators score by catching prey. Both teams learn at the same time. The learner watches and explains how each side adapts to the other; it may never look "solved", and that is acceptable.
248	
249	**Requirements**
250	- **PREY-01** The arena is a square with walls, 6 prey, 2 predators and 10 food pellets; an eaten pellet reappears at a random spot after 50 steps.
251	  - Given seed 5, when an episode starts, then the counts are 6, 2 and 10, with no two objects touching.
252	- **PREY-02** Prey and predators move by smooth pushes as in FISH-02; predators' top speed is 1.2 × the prey's.
253	  - Given both at full speed, when their speeds are read, then the predator's is 1.2 × the prey's.
254	- **PREY-03** A prey touching a pellet eats it; a predator touching a prey catches it and the prey leaves the episode.
255	  - Given a predator touching a prey, when the step ends, then the prey is gone and the predator's catch count is up by 1.
256	- **PREY-04** An episode lasts 1,000 steps or until every prey is caught.
257	  - Given all 6 prey caught at step 600, when the step ends, then the episode is over.
258	- **PREY-05** Each team has its own score: prey score = pellets eaten per prey (higher is better); predator score = prey caught per episode (higher is better).
259	  - Given 18 pellets eaten and 3 prey caught, when the episode ends, then the prey score is 3 and the predator score is 3.
260	- **PREY-06** Every agent observes itself, and the nearest 2 food pellets, nearest 2 prey and nearest 2 predators within a radius of one third of the arena's width; anything outside reads "not seen".
261	  - Given a predator beyond the radius, when a prey's observation is taken, then that predator's entries read "not seen".
262	- **PREY-07** Rule-based baselines: prey flee the nearest seen predator, else move toward the nearest seen pellet; predators chase the nearest seen prey, else wander randomly.
263	  - Given a prey that sees a predator to its right, when the rule acts, then it pushes left.
264	- **PREY-08** Each trained team is evaluated against fixed opponents: trained prey against the rule-based predators, compared with random and rule-based prey; trained predators against the rule-based prey, compared with random and rule-based predators. Each passes as in EVAL-04 and EVAL-05.
265	  - Given 5 finished runs, when evaluated, then a separate verdict is shown for the prey team and the predator team.
266	- **PREY-09** The learner can play any prey checkpoint against any predator checkpoint from the same run, and see a grid of mean scores for every pairing of the 11 checkpoints of each side.
267	  - Given a finished run, when the pairing grid is made, then it shows an 11 × 11 grid of prey scores and of predator scores, each cell the mean of 20 held-out episodes.
268	- **PREY-10** The learning curve shows both teams' scores on the same step axis, so the back-and-forth between them is visible.
269	  - Given 5 finished runs, when the chart is made, then both teams' mean lines and bands appear against the same environment-steps axis.
270	- **PREY-11** Predator-prey runs through the same training runs, logging, replay viewer and evaluation as snake and fish tank, and their results reproduce unchanged after predator-prey is added.
271	  - Given a snake run and a fish run repeated with their original settings and seeds, when they finish, then their results match the originals exactly.
272	
273	**Edge cases & errors**
274	- One team stops improving or collapses during co-training (e.g. predators catch nothing for the last half of the budget) → the run still finishes and the summary flags the collapse with the step it began; it is a result to explain, not an error.
275	- All prey are caught early in most episodes → prey score is still computed per starting prey, so it is never inflated by fewer survivors.
276	
277	## Quality requirements
278	- **QR-01** A single training run of any world and variant finishes within 60 minutes on the learner's laptop (Apple Silicon, CPU only).
279	  - Checked by the run's logged start and end times.
280	- **QR-02** A batch of 5 runs of one world and variant finishes within 90 minutes on the same laptop, running in parallel.
281	  - Checked by the batch's first start and last end time.
282	- **QR-03** Rented GPU time, if a world truly needs it, costs at most $10 per world, and is used only after a laptop run is shown to miss QR-01.
283	  - Checked against the rental bill, recorded in the world's write-up.
284	- **QR-04** The replay viewer plays at least 30 steps per second at 1× on the laptop, for every world.
285	  - Checked by timing a 1,000-step replay.
286	- **QR-05** Final evaluation (EVAL-03 and EVAL-04) of one world and variant finishes within 10 minutes on the laptop.
287	  - Checked by timing the evaluation.
288	- **QR-06** Everything runs locally with no network connection once installed.
289	  - Checked by running a full train, evaluate and replay cycle with networking off.
290	
291	## Assumptions
292	- "About an hour of training per world" means one run ≤ 60 minutes, with the 5 runs needed for evaluation running in parallel.
293	- "Several training runs" means 5 runs with different seeds.
294	- "Clearly beats" means significant at 5% after correction, and every single run beats the rule baseline.
295	- "Write the core learning algorithms myself" means the learner writes the learning algorithms; automatic gradient computation is allowed.
296	- The reference check is an 80% bar, not a requirement to match a library.
297	- Fish and predator-prey both use smooth movement; predator-prey reuses the fish movement rules.
298	- The world sizes, counts and step limits above are starting values the learner may tune before a stage's build.
299

304	| 2026-10-17 | Checkpoint count fixed to 11 (0%–100% every 10%), as its example listed; pairing grid follows | routed from test-planning | TRAIN-03, PREY-09 (changed) |

1	# Milestone shape: interface, step spines, wiring, system
2	
3	Read when writing a milestone's tasks. Examples: `examples-phase0.md`.
4	
5	A milestone is planned from its pipelines' steps: each step has one owner, one module and typed `In → Out`, so it is the unit of work. The plan freezes every boundary between steps first, so the work on both sides starts at once instead of queueing behind one spine. Every task carries `**Kind:**`:
6	
7	```
8	## Phase 0: Interface              interface (solo, first)       types, signatures, samples, fakes, exports, __main__
9	## Phase 1: Step spines            spine, one per step           each writes its owner module only
10	## Phase 2: Wiring and thicken     wiring, one per pipeline      its entry module, smoke test
11	                                   thicken (tasks.md)            each after its step's spine
12	## Phase 3: System                 system (solo, last)           fakes swapped for real providers
13	```
14	
15	Phases name the usual waves; the dependency graph (`tasks.md` step 9) decides what may start. Tester halves of split tasks start right after the interface.
16	
17	**a. Interface task** (`**Kind:** interface`, `**Step:** contract`, solo, `**Depends on:** —`). It freezes, as code, every boundary in the milestone whose two sides belong to different tasks:
18	- each contract `type` section the milestone's steps pass (`In → Out`), written into its owner's `types.py`, with a schema (pandera for a frame, JSON Schema across runtimes) and one sample per case in `tests/contracts/<component>.<Type>/`. A case naming a probe sample (`PB-01 samples/sync_added.json#0`) puts `PB-01:samples` in `Read:`; the task derives the type's sample from it, never copies it
19	- each new step's signature in its owner module, with the body `raise NotImplementedError("<task id>")` naming the task that replaces it
20	- for each function a pipeline calls from another pipeline or package (its `**Boundary:**` line): a fixture-backed fake serving its fixtures, where the caller's tests load it. A `call` edge to a contract symbol is mitigated by that fake; `none` there needs a reason (`dt lint`)
21	- for each object with behavior a step passes (a Protocol or class: `core.Dataset`, `runstore.RunWriter`) and each call into another component's function: the Protocol or signature plus a fixture-backed replay double in `tests/contracts/<component>.<Type>/`. The double is that type's sample, not a fake: the system task never swaps it
22	- each component's `__init__.py` exports, and the package's `__main__.py`: the dispatch from each pipeline's trigger to its entry function, whose signature it stubs naming the wiring task, in that pipeline's own entry module
23	
24	Not frozen: helpers, private functions, anything one task owns end to end, and boundaries an earlier milestone froze. `Read:` the type and boundary symbols. Acceptance: every sample validates against its type; every signature imports; each fake and replay double serves its fixtures. `Writes:` each component folder as one glob (`src/gridload/profile/**`) plus its contract folders; `task-size` doesn't count them. A milestone across packages may have one interface task per provider, all solo and first; interface tasks never depend on each other. When one runtime's types are generated from another's schema, the providing interface task runs the generator and writes the generated file too (its `Writes:` claims it).
25	
26	**b. Step spines** (`**Kind:** spine`, `**Step:** PL-NN.<function>`, solo, XS or S). One per new step, each depending only on the interface task: it replaces the stub naming it with the step's spine version from the step table. `Writes:` its owner module and that module's unit test, nothing else; steps sharing one module share one spine task. Acceptance: the step's input sample gives a schema-valid output; the signature is unchanged. A step another pipeline provides (`PL-01 as built`) gets no spine.
27	
28	**c. Wiring** (`**Kind:** wiring`, `**Step:** PL-NN`, solo). One per new pipeline: the entry function in the pipeline's own entry module (the layout's, never one module shared by several pipelines), so wiring tasks run side by side, and its smoke test. `__main__.py` is the interface task's. It depends on its pipeline's spines, since the smoke runs them (`order`, `none: integration: …`), never on their thicken tasks. A call to another pipeline or package runs against that boundary's fake, so wiring PL-02 never waits for PL-01. Acceptance: the smoke command runs end to end and is committed as the pipeline's smoke test; the pipeline's pre-build sections are replaced by a `dt layout` pointer (`dt land` runs `dt prebuild-clear` when the wiring lands).
29	
30	**d. System task** (`**Kind:** system`, solo, last). It swaps each fake for the real provider, in the entry its wiring wrote (a `file` edge), and runs every smoke command and the milestone's golden cases on the real chain; the boundary samples still validate against real output. Multi-package: one per new system pipeline, `**Step:**` that pipeline, `**Package:**` the owner of its first step, `**Reason:** system task`; the root has no pre-build sections, so no `dt layout` bullet. Single package: `**Step:**` the calling pipeline. A milestone with no fake has no system task.
31	
32	**Folds** keep the task count down. The pipeline's wiring depends on a fold task, so a fold is allowed only where that task is XS and the critical path doesn't grow. Record each on its task as `**Reason:** fold: <rule>`:
33	- A step with a golden case whose spine and thicken are both in the milestone, or whose spine version is its whole behavior, may be one split thicken task that replaces the stub itself; with a thicken folded in, its acceptance keeps the baseline selectable.
34	- A trivial step (pass-through, print) is built by its pipeline's wiring task; its stub names the wiring task.
35	
36	**Fakes and stubs.** A fake is allowed only where a pipeline calls another pipeline or package: both sides test against the same fixtures, so neither drifts from the contract unseen. Mocking a step is forbidden: a step's spine is the real baseline, and its consumer builds against the type's sample (for a Protocol or class, its replay double). The interface stubs are the floor's one stub carve-out (`build-setup`'s CR-02): allowed while the task they name is open; the gate fails on one naming a done task, the milestone exit on any.
37	
38	**Frozen means frozen.** Changing a frozen type, sample, signature or fake is a `contract-change`: `architecture-design` Revise, then a new interface task naming the symbol in `Read:`, never an edit to make a test pass. The bar enforces it (CR-09): once a contract folder exists at a task's base the implementer can't write it, and `dt guard` asks before any edit no open interface task covers. The re-freeze is that interface task, run by an implementer agent, never the main session: its `Writes:` names the frozen folders it changes, which opens them to it alone.
39

wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-all-at-once-A3-r1/with_skill/project/.claude/worktrees/agent-aaf9dad3f1ec1ce1f/src/rllab/core/types.py
"""Shared types: world and policy protocols, run settings, held-out seed sets (contract: core)."""

from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Literal, Protocol

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

Direction = Literal["higher", "lower"]
Settings = Mapping[str, int | float | str | bool]
Array = npt.NDArray[Any]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Discrete:
    """A choice from `n` actions: ints in `[0, n)`."""

    n: int


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Box:
    """`dims` floats, each in `[low, high]`."""

    dims: int
    low: float
    high: float


ActionSpace = Discrete | Box


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TeamSpec:
    name: str
    agents: tuple[int, ...]
    score_name: str
    direction: Direction


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MeasureSpec:
    name: str
    team: str
    direction: Direction


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class WorldSpec:
    world: str
    variant: str
    agents: tuple[str, ...]
    teams: tuple[TeamSpec, ...]
    obs_len: int
    action: ActionSpace
    max_steps: int
    measures: tuple[MeasureSpec, ...]
    sight: float | None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Step:
    obs: Array
    rewards: Array
    active: Array
    done: bool
    truncated: bool


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EpisodeResult:
    seed: int
    length: int
    scores: Mapping[str, float]
    team_rewards: Mapping[str, float]
    measures: Mapping[str, float]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Drawable:
    kind: str
    x: float
    y: float
    size: float
    team: str | None
    agent: int | None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DrawState:
    width: float
    height: float
    objects: tuple[Drawable, ...]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Frame:
    step: int
    draw: DrawState
    scores: Mapping[str, float]
    rewards: Array


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Episode:
    result: EpisodeResult
    frames: tuple[Frame, ...] | None
    hit_step_limit: bool


class World(Protocol):
    spec: WorldSpec

    def reset(self, seed: int) -> Step: ...

    def step(self, actions: Array) -> Step: ...

    def result(self) -> EpisodeResult: ...

    def draw(self) -> DrawState: ...


class Policy(Protocol):
    def act(self, obs: Array, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Array: ...


class ActionError(ValueError):
    """An action outside `WorldSpec.action`; worlds refuse it, never clip it."""

    def __init__(self, agent: str, value: object) -> None:
        super().__init__(f"agent {agent!r} sent an action outside the declared format: {value!r}")
        self.agent = agent
        self.value = value


EVAL_SEEDS: tuple[int, ...] = tuple(range(1_000_000, 1_000_020))
FINAL_SEEDS: tuple[int, ...] = tuple(range(2_000_000, 2_000_100))


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Output:
    path: Path
    kind: Literal["chart", "video", "writeup"]
    made_from: tuple[str, ...]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MethodConfig:
    method: Literal["dqn", "ppo"]
    settings: Settings


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RunConfig:
    name: str
    world: str
    variant: str
    methods: Mapping[str, MethodConfig]
    seed: int
    budget: int
    check_settings: Mapping[str, Settings]
    device: str = "cpu"

"""JSON form of the contract types: dataclasses as objects, arrays as nested lists.

`to_json` and `from_json` are inverse on every contract dataclass. Decoding follows the type
hints: a union takes the first member the value decodes as, so `Discrete | Box` needs no tag.
JSON arrays decode as float32 when they hold floats, int64 for ints and bool for booleans,
the dtypes `core.Step` declares.
"""

import types
from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import fields, is_dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timedelta
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Literal, Union, cast, get_args, get_origin, get_type_hints

import numpy as np

_SCALARS: dict[type, type] = {int: int, float: float, str: str, bool: bool}


def to_json(value: object) -> Any:
    """The JSON-ready form of a contract value."""
    if is_dataclass(value) and not isinstance(value, type):
        return {f.name: to_json(getattr(value, f.name)) for f in fields(value)}
    if isinstance(value, Mapping):
        return {str(k): to_json(v) for k, v in value.items()}
    if isinstance(value, (tuple, list)):
        return [to_json(v) for v in value]
    if isinstance(value, (np.ndarray, np.generic)):
        return value.tolist()
    if isinstance(value, Path):
        return str(value)
    if isinstance(value, datetime):
        return value.isoformat()
    if isinstance(value, timedelta):
        return value.total_seconds()
    return value


def from_json[T](tp: type[T], data: object) -> T:
    """Rebuild a value of type `tp` from its JSON form; `ValueError` when it doesn't fit."""
    return cast(T, decode(tp, data))


def decode(tp: object, data: object) -> object:
    """`from_json` for any type hint, including unions and aliases."""
    origin = get_origin(tp)
    if origin is Literal:
        return _literal(get_args(tp), data)
    if origin in {Union, types.UnionType}:
        return _union(get_args(tp), data)
    if origin is not None:
        return _generic(origin, get_args(tp), data)
    if isinstance(tp, type) and is_dataclass(tp):
        return _dataclass(tp, data)
    return _leaf(tp, data)


def _literal(options: tuple[object, ...], data: object) -> object:
    if data not in options:
        raise ValueError(f"{data!r} is not one of {options}")
    return data


def _union(members: tuple[object, ...], data: object) -> object:
    if data is None and type(None) in members:
        return None
    for member in members:
        try:
            return decode(member, data)
        except (TypeError, ValueError):
            continue
    raise ValueError(f"{data!r} fits none of {members}")


def _generic(origin: object, args: tuple[object, ...], data: object) -> object:
    if origin is np.ndarray:
        return _array(data)
    if isinstance(origin, type) and issubclass(origin, Mapping):
        return {_text(k): decode(args[1], v) for k, v in _object(data).items()}
    items = _list(data)
    if origin is tuple and args[-1:] == (Ellipsis,):
        return tuple(decode(args[0], v) for v in items)
    if origin is tuple:
        if len(items) != len(args):
            raise ValueError(f"expected {len(args)} items, got {len(items)}")
        return tuple(decode(a, v) for a, v in zip(args, items, strict=True))
    return [decode(args[0], v) for v in items]


def _dataclass(cls: type, data: object) -> object:
    values = _object(data)
    hints = get_type_hints(cls)
    names = {f.name for f in fields(cls)}
    if extra := set(values) - names:
        raise ValueError(f"{cls.__name__} has no field(s) {sorted(extra)}")
    return cls(**{name: decode(hints[name], v) for name, v in values.items()})


def _leaf(tp: object, data: object) -> object:
    if tp in {object, Any}:
        return data
    if tp is type(None):
        return _literal((None,), data)
    if tp is np.ndarray:
        return _array(data)
    if tp is Path:
        return Path(_text(data))
    if tp is datetime:
        return datetime.fromisoformat(_text(data))
    if tp is timedelta:
        return timedelta(seconds=_number(data))
    if tp is float:
        return _number(data)
    if tp in _SCALARS and type(data) is tp:
        return data
    raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not a {tp}")


def _array(data: object) -> np.ndarray[Any, Any]:
    array = np.asarray(_list(data))
    if array.dtype.kind == "f":
        return array.astype(np.float32)
    if array.dtype.kind in {"i", "u"}:
        return array.astype(np.int64)
    if array.dtype.kind == "b" or array.size == 0:
        return array
    raise TypeError(f"array of {array.dtype} is not numeric")


def _number(data: object) -> float:
    if isinstance(data, bool) or not isinstance(data, (int, float)):
        raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not a number")
    return float(data)


def _text(data: object) -> str:
    if not isinstance(data, str):
        raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not a string")
    return data


def _object(data: object) -> dict[str, object]:
    if not isinstance(data, dict):
        raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not an object")
    return cast(dict[str, object], data)


def _list(data: object) -> list[object]:
    if not isinstance(data, list):
        raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not a list")
    return cast(list[object], data)

_ENCODERS: tuple[tuple[type | tuple[type, ...], Callable[[Any], object]], ...] = (
    ((np.ndarray, np.generic), lambda v: v.tolist()),
    (Path, str),
    (datetime, lambda v: v.isoformat()),
    (timedelta, lambda v: v.total_seconds()),
)


def to_json(value: object) -> Any:
    """The JSON-ready form of a contract value."""
    if is_dataclass(value) and not isinstance(value, type):
        return {f.name: to_json(getattr(value, f.name)) for f in fields(value)}
    if isinstance(value, Mapping):
        return {str(k): to_json(v) for k, v in value.items()}
    if isinstance(value, (tuple, list)):
        return [to_json(v) for v in value]
    return next((encode(value) for kinds, encode in _ENCODERS if isinstance(value, kinds)), value)

"""JSON form of the contract types: dataclasses as objects, arrays as nested lists.

`to_json` and `from_json` are inverse on every contract dataclass. Decoding follows the type
hints: a union takes the first member the value decodes as, so `Discrete | Box` needs no tag.
JSON arrays decode as float32 when they hold floats, int64 for ints and bool for booleans,
the dtypes `core.Step` declares.
"""

import types
from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping
from dataclasses import fields, is_dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timedelta
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Literal, Union, cast, get_args, get_origin, get_type_hints

import numpy as np

_ENCODERS: tuple[tuple[type | tuple[type, ...], Callable[[Any], object]], ...] = (
    ((np.ndarray, np.generic), lambda v: v.tolist()),
    (Path, str),
    (datetime, lambda v: v.isoformat()),
    (timedelta, lambda v: v.total_seconds()),
)


def to_json(value: object) -> Any:
    """The JSON-ready form of a contract value."""
    if is_dataclass(value) and not isinstance(value, type):
        return {f.name: to_json(getattr(value, f.name)) for f in fields(value)}
    if isinstance(value, Mapping):
        return {str(k): to_json(v) for k, v in value.items()}
    if isinstance(value, (tuple, list)):
        return [to_json(v) for v in value]
    return next((encode(value) for kinds, encode in _ENCODERS if isinstance(value, kinds)), value)


def from_json[T](tp: type[T], data: object) -> T:
    """Rebuild a value of type `tp` from its JSON form; `ValueError` when it doesn't fit."""
    return cast(T, decode(tp, data))


def decode(tp: object, data: object) -> object:
    """`from_json` for any type hint, including unions and aliases."""
    origin = get_origin(tp)
    if origin is Literal:
        return _literal(get_args(tp), data)
    if origin in {Union, types.UnionType}:
        return _union(get_args(tp), data)
    if origin is not None:
        return _generic(origin, get_args(tp), data)
    if isinstance(tp, type) and is_dataclass(tp):
        return _dataclass(tp, data)
    return _leaf(tp, data)


def _literal(options: tuple[object, ...], data: object) -> object:
    if data not in options:
        raise ValueError(f"{data!r} is not one of {options}")
    return data


def _union(members: tuple[object, ...], data: object) -> object:
    if data is None and type(None) in members:
        return None
    for member in members:
        try:
            return decode(member, data)
        except (TypeError, ValueError):
            continue
    raise ValueError(f"{data!r} fits none of {members}")


def _generic(origin: object, args: tuple[object, ...], data: object) -> object:
    if origin is np.ndarray:
        return _array(data)
    if isinstance(origin, type) and issubclass(origin, Mapping):
        return {_text(k): decode(args[1], v) for k, v in _object(data).items()}
    items = _list(data)
    if origin is tuple and args[-1:] == (Ellipsis,):
        return tuple(decode(args[0], v) for v in items)
    if origin is tuple:
        if len(items) != len(args):
            raise ValueError(f"expected {len(args)} items, got {len(items)}")
        return tuple(decode(a, v) for a, v in zip(args, items, strict=True))
    return [decode(args[0], v) for v in items]


def _dataclass(cls: type, data: object) -> object:
    values = _object(data)
    hints = get_type_hints(cls)
    names = {f.name for f in fields(cls)}
    if extra := set(values) - names:
        raise ValueError(f"{cls.__name__} has no field(s) {sorted(extra)}")
    return cls(**{name: decode(hints[name], v) for name, v in values.items()})


def _leaf(tp: object, data: object) -> object:
    if (decoder := _DECODERS.get(tp)) is not None:
        return decoder(data)
    if tp in {int, str, bool} and type(data) is tp:
        return data
    raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not a {tp}")


def _array(data: object) -> np.ndarray[Any, Any]:
    array = np.asarray(_list(data))
    if array.dtype.kind == "f":
        return array.astype(np.float32)
    if array.dtype.kind in {"i", "u"}:
        return array.astype(np.int64)
    if array.dtype.kind == "b" or array.size == 0:
        return array
    raise TypeError(f"array of {array.dtype} is not numeric")


def _number(data: object) -> float:
    if isinstance(data, bool) or not isinstance(data, (int, float)):
        raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not a number")
    return float(data)


def _text(data: object) -> str:
    if not isinstance(data, str):
        raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not a string")
    return data


def _object(data: object) -> dict[str, object]:
    if not isinstance(data, dict):
        raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not an object")
    return cast(dict[str, object], data)


def _list(data: object) -> list[object]:
    if not isinstance(data, list):
        raise TypeError(f"{data!r} is not a list")
    return cast(list[object], data)


def _same(data: object) -> object:
    return data


def _none(data: object) -> object:
    return _literal((None,), data)


_DECODERS: dict[object, Callable[[object], object]] = {
    object: _same,
    Any: _same,
    type(None): _none,
    np.ndarray: _array,
    Path: lambda d: Path(_text(d)),
    datetime: lambda d: datetime.fromisoformat(_text(d)),
    timedelta: lambda d: timedelta(seconds=_number(d)),
    float: _number,
}
full input and output

  "content": "\"\"\"JSON form of the contract types: dataclasses as objects, arrays as nested lists.\n\n`to_json` and `from_json` are inverse on every contract dataclass. Decoding follows the type\nhints: a union takes the first member the value decodes as, so `Discrete | Box` needs no tag.\nJSON arrays decode as float32 when they hold floats, int64 for ints and bool for booleans,\nthe dtypes `core.Step` declares.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport types\nfrom collections.abc import Callable, Mapping\nfrom dataclasses import fields, is_dataclass\nfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any, Literal, Union, cast, get_args, get_origin, get_type_hints\n\nimport numpy as np\n\n_ENCODERS: tuple[tuple[type | tuple[type, ...], Callable[[Any], object]], ...] = (\n    ((np.ndarray, np.generic), lambda v: v.tolist()),\n    (Path, str),\n    (datetime, lambda v: v.isoformat()),\n    (timedelta, lambda v: v.total_seconds()),\n)\n\n\ndef to_json(value: object) -> Any:\n    \"\"\"The JSON-ready form of a contract value.\"\"\"\n    if is_dataclass(value) and not isinstance(value, type):\n        return {f.name: to_json(getattr(value, f.name)) for f in fields(value)}\n    if isinstance(value, Mapping):\n        return {str(k): to_json(v) for k, v in value.items()}\n    if isinstance(value, (tuple, list)):\n        return [to_json(v) for v in value]\n    return next((encode(value) for kinds, encode in _ENCODERS if isinstance(value, kinds)), value)\n\n\ndef from_json[T](tp: type[T], data: object) -> T:\n    \"\"\"Rebuild a value of type `tp` from its JSON form; `ValueError` when it doesn't fit.\"\"\"\n    return cast(T, decode(tp, data))\n\n\ndef decode(tp: object, data: object) -> object:\n    \"\"\"`from_json` for any type hint, including unions and aliases.\"\"\"\n    origin = get_origin(tp)\n    if origin is Literal:\n        return _literal(get_args(tp), data)\n    if origin in {Union, types.UnionType}:\n        return _union(get_args(tp), data)\n    if origin is not None:\n        return _generic(origin, get_args(tp), data)\n    if isinstance(tp, type) and is_dataclass(tp):\n        return _dataclass(tp, data)\n    return _leaf(tp, data)\n\n\ndef _literal(options: tuple[object, ...], data: object) -> object:\n    if data not in options:\n        raise ValueError(f\"{data!r} is not one of {options}\")\n    return data\n\n\ndef _union(members: tuple[object, ...], data: object) -> object:\n    if data is None and type(None) in members:\n        return None\n    for member in members:\n        try:\n            return decode(member, data)\n        except (TypeError, ValueError):\n            continue\n    raise ValueError(f\"{data!r} fits none of {members}\")\n\n\ndef _generic(origin: object, args: tuple[object, ...], data: object) -> object:\n    if origin is np.ndarray:\n        return _array(data)\n    if isinstance(origin, type) and issubclass(origin, Mapping):\n        return {_text(k): decode(args[1], v) for k, v in _object(data).items()}\n    items = _list(data)\n    if origin is tuple and args[-1:] == (Ellipsis,):\n        return tuple(decode(args[0], v) for v in items)\n    if origin is tuple:\n        if len(items) != len(args):\n            raise ValueError(f\"expected {len(args)} items, got {len(items)}\")\n        return tuple(decode(a, v) for a, v in zip(args, items, strict=True))\n    return [decode(args[0], v) for v in items]\n\n\ndef _dataclass(cls: type, data: object) -> object:\n    values = _object(data)\n    hints = get_type_hints(cls)\n    names = {f.name for f in fields(cls)}\n    if extra := set(values) - names:\n        raise ValueError(f\"{cls.__name__} has no field(s) {sorted(extra)}\")\n    return cls(**{name: decode(hints[name], v) for name, v in values.items()})\n\n\ndef _leaf(tp: object, data: object) -> object:\n    if (decoder := _DECODERS.get(tp)) is not None:\n        return decoder(data)\n    if tp in {int, str, bool} and type(data) is tp:\n        return data\n    raise TypeError(f\"{data!r} is not a {tp}\")\n\n\ndef _array(data: object) -> np.ndarray[Any, Any]:\n    array = np.asarray(_list(data))\n    if array.dtype.kind == \"f\":\n        return array.astype(np.float32)\n    if array.dtype.kind in {\"i\", \"u\"}:\n        return array.astype(np.int64)\n    if array.dtype.kind == \"b\" or array.size == 0:\n        return array\n    raise TypeError(f\"array of {array.dtype} is not numeric\")\n\n\ndef _number(data: object) -> float:\n    if isinstance(data, bool) or not isinstance(data, (int, float)):\n        raise TypeError(f\"{data!r} is not a number\")\n    return float(data)\n\n\ndef _text(data: object) -> str:\n    if not isinstance(data, str):\n        raise TypeError(f\"{data!r} is not a string\")\n    return data\n\n\ndef _object(data: object) -> dict[str, object]:\n    if not isinstance(data, dict):\n        raise TypeError(f\"{data!r} is not an object\")\n    return cast(dict[str, object], data)\n\n\ndef _list(data: object) -> list[object]:\n    if not isinstance(data, list):\n        raise TypeError(f\"{data!r} is not a list\")\n    return cast(list[object], data)\n\n\ndef _same(data: object) -> object:\n    return data\n\n\ndef _none(data: object) -> object:\n    return _literal((None,), data)\n\n\n_DECODERS: dict[object, Callable[[object], object]] = {\n    object: _same,\n    Any: _same,\n    type(None): _none,\n    np.ndarray: _array,\n    Path: lambda d: Path(_text(d)),\n    datetime: lambda d: datetime.fromisoformat(_text(d)),\n    timedelta: lambda d: timedelta(seconds=_number(d)),\n    float: _number,\n}\n"

"""Replay doubles and fakes for the core contract, serving `tests/contracts/core.*/` fixtures.

A replay double plays back one recorded interaction and refuses any other, so a consumer's test
proves it drives the protocol the way the recording did. Doubles read their recording from the
contract folder of a source checkout (`contract_fixture`); they are test doubles, not providers.
"""

import json
from collections.abc import Mapping
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import numpy as np

from rllab.core.codec import from_json
from rllab.core.types import Array, DrawState, Episode, EpisodeResult, Policy, Step, World, WorldSpec

CONTRACTS = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "tests" / "contracts"


def contract_fixture(folder: str, name: str) -> Any:
    """The JSON fixture `name` in `tests/contracts/<folder>/`."""
    return json.loads((CONTRACTS / folder / name).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))


class ReplayWorld:
    """A `World` that replays one recorded episode: same seed, same actions, same steps."""

    spec: WorldSpec

    def __init__(self, recording: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
        self.spec = from_json(WorldSpec, recording["spec"])
        self.seed: int = recording["seed"]
        self.actions = [from_json(np.ndarray, a) for a in recording["actions"]]
        self._steps = [from_json(Step, s) for s in recording["steps"]]
        self._draws = [from_json(DrawState, d) for d in recording["draws"]]
        self._result = from_json(EpisodeResult, recording["result"])
        self._t = -1

    def reset(self, seed: int) -> Step:
        if seed != self.seed:
            raise ValueError(f"recorded from seed {self.seed}, not {seed}")
        self._t = 0
        return self._steps[0]

    def step(self, actions: Array) -> Step:
        if self._t < 0 or self._steps[self._t].done:
            raise RuntimeError("step before reset or after the episode ended")
        expected = self.actions[self._t]
        if not np.array_equal(actions, expected):
            raise ValueError(f"step {self._t + 1}: actions {actions!r}, recorded {expected!r}")
        self._t += 1
        return self._steps[self._t]

    def result(self) -> EpisodeResult:
        return self._result

    def draw(self) -> DrawState:
        return self._draws[self._t]


class ReplayPolicy:
    """A `Policy` that answers each recorded observation, in order, with its recorded actions."""

    def __init__(self, recording: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
        self.calls = [
            (from_json(np.ndarray, c["obs"]), from_json(np.ndarray, c["actions"]))
            for c in recording["calls"]
        ]
        self._next = 0

    def act(self, obs: Array, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Array:
        if self._next >= len(self.calls):
            raise ValueError(f"the recording holds only {len(self.calls)} calls")
        expected, actions = self.calls[self._next]
        if not np.array_equal(obs, expected):
            raise ValueError(f"call {self._next}: observation differs from the recording")
        self._next += 1
        return actions


def snake_world() -> World:
    """The recorded seed-42 snake episode (`tests/contracts/core.World/`)."""
    return ReplayWorld(contract_fixture("core.World", "recording.json"))


def snake_rule() -> Policy:
    """The snake rule over the 5 observations of the seed-42 episode (`core.Policy`)."""
    return ReplayPolicy(contract_fixture("core.Policy", "recording.json"))


def fake_play_episode(
    world: World, policies: Mapping[str, Policy], seed: int, *, trace: bool = False
) -> Episode:
    """`core.play_episode` fake: the recorded snake-rule episode on seed 7."""
    episode = from_json(Episode, contract_fixture("core.play_episode", "fake.json")["returns"])
    if seed != episode.result.seed:
        raise ValueError(f"the fake serves seed {episode.result.seed}, not {seed}")
    return episode if trace else Episode(episode.result, None, episode.hit_step_limit)

"""Replay doubles and fakes for the worlds contract, serving `tests/contracts/worlds.*/`."""

from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import Any, Literal

import numpy as np

from rllab.core.codec import from_json
from rllab.core.doubles import ReplayWorld, contract_fixture, snake_world
from rllab.core.types import Array, World
from rllab.worlds.types import CheckWorld


class ReplayCheckWorld(ReplayWorld):
    """A `CheckWorld` replaying one recorded episode, with its states and best actions."""

    def __init__(self, recording: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
        super().__init__(recording)
        self._states = from_json(np.ndarray, recording["states"])
        self._best = from_json(np.ndarray, recording["best_actions"])

    def states(self) -> Array:
        return self._states

    def best_actions(self) -> Array:
        return self._best


def check_world(kind: Literal["discrete", "continuous"]) -> CheckWorld:
    """The recorded two-exit corridor (`discrete`) or 1-D target point (`continuous`)."""
    return ReplayCheckWorld(contract_fixture("worlds.CheckWorld", f"{kind}.json"))


def fake_make(world: str, variant: str = "default", **overrides: int | float) -> World:
    """`worlds.make` fake: the recorded seed-42 snake double for `make("snake")`."""
    if (world, variant) != ("snake", "default") or overrides:
        raise ValueError(f"the fake serves only snake/default, not {world}/{variant} {overrides}")
    return snake_world()

"""Replay doubles and fakes for the baselines contract, serving `tests/contracts/baselines.*/`."""

from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import Any, Literal

from rllab.baselines.types import BaselineMeans, RulePolicy
from rllab.core.codec import from_json
from rllab.core.doubles import ReplayPolicy, contract_fixture


class ReplayRulePolicy(ReplayPolicy):
    """A `RulePolicy` replaying recorded observations and actions, with its description."""

    def __init__(self, recording: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
        super().__init__(recording)
        self.description: str = recording["description"]


def rule_policy(world: Literal["snake", "fish"]) -> RulePolicy:
    """The recorded snake rule over 5 observations, or the fish rule seeing no ball."""
    return ReplayRulePolicy(contract_fixture("baselines.RulePolicy", f"{world}.json"))


def fake_rule_for(world: str, variant: str, team: str) -> RulePolicy:
    """`baselines.rule_for` fake: the recorded snake rule double."""
    if (world, variant, team) != ("snake", "default", "snake"):
        raise KeyError(f"the fake has no rule for {world}/{variant} team {team}")
    return rule_policy("snake")


def fake_baseline_means(world: str, variant: str) -> BaselineMeans:
    """`baselines.baseline_means` fake: the snake `BaselineMeans`."""
    if (world, variant) != ("snake", "default"):
        raise KeyError(f"the fake has no baselines for {world}/{variant}")
    fixture = contract_fixture("baselines.baseline_means", "fake.json")
    return from_json(BaselineMeans, fixture["returns"])

"""Learner types (contract: methods)."""

from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

import numpy as np

from rllab.core.types import Array, Policy


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Transition:
    obs: Array
    actions: Array
    rewards: Array
    next_obs: Array
    done: bool
    truncated: bool
    active: Array


class Learner(Protocol):
    def act(self, obs: Array, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Array: ...

    def observe(self, t: Transition) -> None: ...

    def policy(self) -> Policy: ...

    def state_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]: ...


class NonFiniteError(ValueError):
    """A loss or parameter became NaN or infinite; raised before any parameter changes."""

    def __init__(self, value: float) -> None:
        super().__init__(f"non-finite value in the learner: {value}")
        self.value = value


class SpecMismatchError(ValueError):
    """A checkpoint's `WorldSpec` no longer matches the world's, naming each differing field."""

    def __init__(self, fields: Sequence[str]) -> None:
        super().__init__(f"checkpoint spec differs from the world in: {', '.join(fields)}")
        self.fields = tuple(fields)

"""Replay doubles and fakes for the methods contract, serving `tests/contracts/methods.*/`."""

from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import Any

import numpy as np

from rllab.core.codec import from_json
from rllab.core.doubles import ReplayPolicy, contract_fixture, snake_rule
from rllab.core.types import Array, Discrete, MethodConfig, Policy, WorldSpec
from rllab.methods.types import Learner, Transition
from rllab.runlog.types import Checkpoint


class ReplayLearner:
    """A `Learner` serving a recorded DQN learner's actions, greedy policy and state.

    `act` answers with the recorded actions in order, starting over when they run out, so a
    training loop longer than the recording can run on it; it refuses an observation whose
    shape differs from the recording's. `observe` keeps every transition in `observed`.
    """

    def __init__(self, recording: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
        self.acts = [
            (from_json(np.ndarray, a["obs"]), from_json(np.ndarray, a["actions"]))
            for a in recording["acts"]
        ]
        self.transitions = [from_json(Transition, t) for t in recording["transitions"]]
        self.observed: list[Transition] = []
        self._policy = recording["policy"]
        self._state: dict[str, object] = recording["state"]
        self._next = 0

    def act(self, obs: Array, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Array:
        expected, actions = self.acts[self._next % len(self.acts)]
        if obs.shape != expected.shape:
            raise ValueError(f"observation shape {obs.shape}, recorded {expected.shape}")
        self._next += 1
        return actions

    def observe(self, t: Transition) -> None:
        self.observed.append(t)

    def policy(self) -> Policy:
        return ReplayPolicy(self._policy)

    def state_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]:
        return dict(self._state)


def dqn_learner() -> Learner:
    """The recorded DQN learner over 10 snake transitions (`tests/contracts/methods.Learner/`)."""
    return ReplayLearner(contract_fixture("methods.Learner", "recording.json"))


def fake_make_learner(
    config: MethodConfig, spec: WorldSpec, team: str, rng: np.random.Generator
) -> Learner:
    """`methods.make_learner` fake: the recorded DQN learner double for a snake team."""
    if config.method != "dqn" or not isinstance(spec.action, Discrete):
        raise ValueError(f"the fake serves dqn on a Discrete action space, not {config.method}")
    if team not in {t.name for t in spec.teams}:
        raise ValueError(f"no team {team!r} in {spec.world}")
    return dqn_learner()


def fake_load_policy(ckpt: Checkpoint, team: str, spec: WorldSpec) -> Policy:
    """`methods.load_policy` fake: the snake rule policy for any snake checkpoint."""
    if ckpt.spec.world != "snake" or spec.world != "snake":
        raise ValueError(f"the fake serves snake checkpoints, not {ckpt.spec.world}")
    return snake_rule()

"""Run folder types (contract: runlog). Every field type comes from `core` (AD-12)."""

from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timedelta
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Literal, Protocol

from rllab.core.types import EpisodeResult, MethodConfig, RunConfig, WorldSpec

RunStatus = Literal["running", "finished", "incomplete", "failed"]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Failure:
    step: int
    value: float
    checkpoint: Path


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RunRecord:
    config: RunConfig
    spec: WorldSpec
    started: datetime
    ended: datetime | None
    status: RunStatus
    failure: Failure | None
    path: Path


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EvalPoint:
    step: int
    scores: Mapping[str, float]
    measures: Mapping[str, float]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Checkpoint:
    run: str
    pct: int
    step: int
    spec: WorldSpec
    methods: Mapping[str, MethodConfig]
    state: Mapping[str, dict[str, object]]
    path: Path


class Run(Protocol):
    record: RunRecord

    def episodes(self) -> list[EpisodeResult]: ...

    def evals(self) -> list[EvalPoint]: ...

    def checkpoints(self) -> list[Checkpoint]: ...


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Progress:
    step: int
    budget: int
    latest: EvalPoint | None
    elapsed: timedelta
    status: str


class RunExistsError(FileExistsError):
    """A run folder with this name already exists; the run is refused before any work."""

    def __init__(self, path: Path) -> None:
        super().__init__(f"run folder already exists: {path}")
        self.path = path

"""Run folder writer: record, episode and evaluation logs, checkpoints (AD-06)."""

from collections.abc import Mapping
from pathlib import Path

from rllab.core.types import EpisodeResult, RunConfig, WorldSpec
from rllab.runlog.types import Checkpoint, EvalPoint, Failure, RunRecord


class RunWriter:
    """Writes `runs/<world>/<variant>/<name>/` under `root`; refuses an existing name."""

    def __init__(self, root: Path, config: RunConfig, spec: WorldSpec) -> None:
        raise NotImplementedError("M1-T09")

    def episode(self, result: EpisodeResult) -> None:
        raise NotImplementedError("M1-T09")

    def eval(self, point: EvalPoint) -> None:
        raise NotImplementedError("M1-T09")

    def checkpoint(self, pct: int, step: int, state: Mapping[str, dict[str, object]]) -> Checkpoint:
        raise NotImplementedError("M1-T09")

    def close(self, status: str, failure: Failure | None = None) -> RunRecord:
        raise NotImplementedError("M1-T09")

"""Replay doubles and fakes for the runlog contract, serving `tests/contracts/runlog.*/`."""

from collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from rllab.core.codec import from_json, to_json
from rllab.core.doubles import contract_fixture
from rllab.core.types import EpisodeResult, RunConfig, WorldSpec
from rllab.runlog.types import Checkpoint, EvalPoint, Failure, Progress, Run, RunRecord


class ReplayRun:
    """A `Run` serving one recorded run folder: record, episodes, evaluations, checkpoints."""

    record: RunRecord

    def __init__(self, recording: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
        self.record = from_json(RunRecord, recording["record"])
        self._episodes = [from_json(EpisodeResult, e) for e in recording["episodes"]]
        self._evals = [from_json(EvalPoint, e) for e in recording["evals"]]
        self._checkpoints = [from_json(Checkpoint, c) for c in recording["checkpoints"]]

    def episodes(self) -> list[EpisodeResult]:
        return list(self._episodes)

    def evals(self) -> list[EvalPoint]:
        return list(self._evals)

    def checkpoints(self) -> list[Checkpoint]:
        return list(self._checkpoints)


def snake_run() -> Run:
    """The finished 3-checkpoint snake run (`tests/contracts/runlog.Run/`)."""
    return ReplayRun(contract_fixture("runlog.Run", "recording.json"))


class ReplayRunWriter:
    """A `RunWriter` stand-in that checks each write against a recorded 3-checkpoint snake run.

    Each call must match the next recorded write (same call, same JSON form of its arguments);
    `checkpoint` and `close` return the recorded `Checkpoint` and `RunRecord`.
    """

    def __init__(self, root: Path, config: RunConfig, spec: WorldSpec) -> None:
        recording = contract_fixture("runlog.RunWriter", "recording.json")
        if to_json(config) != recording["config"] or to_json(spec) != recording["spec"]:
            raise ValueError("config or spec differs from the recorded run")
        self.root = root
        self._writes: list[dict[str, Any]] = recording["writes"]
        self._next = 0

    def episode(self, result: EpisodeResult) -> None:
        self._expect({"call": "episode", "result": to_json(result)})

    def eval(self, point: EvalPoint) -> None:
        self._expect({"call": "eval", "point": to_json(point)})

    def checkpoint(self, pct: int, step: int, state: Mapping[str, dict[str, object]]) -> Checkpoint:
        args = {"call": "checkpoint", "pct": pct, "step": step, "state": to_json(state)}
        return from_json(Checkpoint, self._expect(args))

    def close(self, status: str, failure: Failure | None = None) -> RunRecord:
        args = {"call": "close", "status": status, "failure": to_json(failure)}
        return from_json(RunRecord, self._expect(args))

    def _expect(self, args: dict[str, Any]) -> Any:
        if self._next >= len(self._writes):
            raise ValueError(f"the recording holds only {len(self._writes)} writes")
        write = self._writes[self._next]
        if {k: write.get(k) for k in args} != args:
            raise ValueError(f"write {self._next} is {args['call']}, recorded {write['call']}")
        self._next += 1
        return write.get("returns")


def fake_find_runs(
    root: Path, world: str, variant: str, *, include: Sequence[str] = ()
) -> list[Run]:
    """`runlog.find_runs` fake: 5 finished snake runs, plus the incomplete one when named."""
    if (world, variant) != ("snake", "default"):
        return []
    runs: list[Run] = [ReplayRun(r) for r in contract_fixture("runlog.find_runs", "fake.json")]
    return [r for r in runs if r.record.status == "finished" or r.record.config.name in include]


def fake_open_run(path: Path) -> Run:
    """`runlog.open_run` fake: the finished 3-checkpoint snake run double."""
    return snake_run()


def fake_progress(path: Path) -> Progress:
    """`runlog.progress` fake: a run at 35% with the 35% point written."""
    return from_json(Progress, contract_fixture("runlog.progress", "fake.json")["returns"])

"""Training types (contract: train). Run and method settings are `core` types (AD-12)."""

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CheckResult:
    method: str
    world: str
    agreement: float
    passed: bool


class CheckFailedError(RuntimeError):
    """A method failed its check world, so the batch is refused before any run starts."""

    def __init__(self, result: CheckResult) -> None:
        super().__init__(
            f"{result.method} failed its check on {result.world}: "
            f"agreement {result.agreement:.2f} < 0.95"
        )
        self.result = result

"""Fakes for the train contract, serving `tests/contracts/train.*/` fixtures.

`fake_run_train` stands in for PL-02 where PL-03 calls it, until the system task swaps in the
real `run_train`; it is a module-level function so a `spawn` worker can import it.
"""

import json
from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import replace
from pathlib import Path

import torch

from rllab.core.codec import from_json, to_json
from rllab.core.doubles import contract_fixture
from rllab.core.types import RunConfig
from rllab.runlog.doubles import ReplayRun
from rllab.runlog.types import RunExistsError, RunRecord
from rllab.train.types import CheckResult


def fake_load_run_configs(path: Path, seeds: Sequence[int]) -> list[RunConfig]:
    """`train.load_run_configs` fake: the snake configs for seeds 1 and 2."""
    served = [from_json(RunConfig, c) for c in _returns("train.load_run_configs")]
    by_seed = {c.seed: c for c in served}
    if missing := sorted(set(seeds) - set(by_seed)):
        raise ValueError(f"the fake serves seeds {sorted(by_seed)}, not {missing}")
    return [by_seed[s] for s in seeds]


def fake_run_check(config_path: Path, seed: int = 0) -> list[CheckResult]:
    """`train.run_check` fake: the passing DQN check."""
    return [from_json(CheckResult, r) for r in _returns("train.run_check")]


def fake_run_train(config: RunConfig, root: Path) -> RunRecord:
    """`train.run_train` fake: writes the recorded finished 3-checkpoint snake run folder.

    The folder is `root/runs/<world>/<variant>/<name>`; the record carries `config` and that path.
    """
    if (config.world, config.variant) != ("snake", "default"):
        raise ValueError(f"the fake serves snake/default, not {config.world}/{config.variant}")
    folder = root / "runs" / config.world / config.variant / config.name
    if folder.exists():
        raise RunExistsError(folder)
    run = ReplayRun(contract_fixture("train.run_train", "fake.json")["run"])
    record = replace(run.record, config=config, path=folder)
    (folder / "checkpoints").mkdir(parents=True)
    (folder / "run.json").write_text(json.dumps(to_json(record), indent=1), encoding="utf-8")
    _write_lines(folder / "episodes.jsonl", [to_json(e) for e in run.episodes()])
    _write_lines(folder / "evals.jsonl", [to_json(e) for e in run.evals()])
    for ckpt in run.checkpoints():
        torch.save(to_json(ckpt), folder / "checkpoints" / f"ckpt_{ckpt.pct}.pt")
    return record


def fake_run_batch(
    config_path: Path,
    seeds: Sequence[int],
    root: Path,
    *,
    workers: int | None = None,
    check_seed: int = 0,
) -> list[RunRecord]:
    """`train.run_batch` fake: 2 finished snake records (seeds 1 and 2)."""
    records = [from_json(RunRecord, r) for r in _returns("train.run_batch")]
    return [r for r in records if r.config.seed in seeds]


def _returns(folder: str) -> list[object]:
    returns: list[object] = contract_fixture(folder, "fake.json")["returns"]
    return returns


def _write_lines(path: Path, rows: list[object]) -> None:
    path.write_text("".join(json.dumps(r) + "\n" for r in rows), encoding="utf-8")
